### 3. Characters found

Character assets live in two kits and exist as **glTF (.gltf + .bin) with external PNG textures**, FBX, and OBJ variants:

- `Modular Character Outfits - Fantasy[Standard]/Exports/glTF (Godot-Unreal)/Outfits/` — assembled outfits: `Female_Peasant.gltf`, `Female_Ranger.gltf`, `Male_Peasant.gltf`, `Male_Ranger.gltf`
- `.../Exports/glTF (Godot-Unreal)/Modular Parts/` — 20 modular part meshes (`Female_Peasant_Arms`, `Female_Ranger_Head_Hood`, etc.); use with the included base-body textures in `Textures/Base` / `Textures/Peasant` / `Textures/Ranger`
- Same characters also exist as FBX (`Exports/FBX (Unity)`) and OBJ variants.
- `Universal Animation Library 2[Standard]/Female Mannequin/` — `Mannequin_F.glb` (Unreal-Godot) + `Mannequin_F.blend` + FBX; shares the rig/proportions with the UAL2 male mannequin so animations retarget cleanly.
- The outfits attach to a base body — per the Readme, only the **head** of the base model is required; full body under clothing clips.
- **Shared skeleton:** every character asset (4 outfits + 20 modular parts + `Mannequin_F.glb` + both UAL2 rig files) is skinned to a **65-joint skeleton** with matching joint counts — UAL2 clips should be directly playable on the outfit meshes with three.js `AnimationMixer` (one-time verification recommended).
- One curiosity: `Modular Parts/Female_Peasant_Body.gltf` ships a baked-in test animation clip `jog_fwd_loop` (the only animated `.gltf` outside the UAL kit).
- Kit totals: 121 files, 291.8 MB.
